People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I’m not really worried about gaining weight. It’s more just… I don’t pay much attention to eating sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating because of concerns or thoughts about the food itself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not really because of the food itself. I just don’t think much about it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in food is related to your mood or how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most mornings I wake up feeling heavy and low, like the day’s already gone dull before I even get dressed.     "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… my eating’s been off. Some days I just forget lunch altogether, and other days I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets even though it doesn’t really taste right. It’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the weight… I haven’t been keeping track. It’s more that my eating’s been strange—forgetting lunch some days, then picking at crackers or sweets when nothing tastes right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're less interested in food than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes. I don’t think about food the same way. Some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’m just picking at crackers or sweets even though it doesn’t taste like much.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think that’s it. I’m not really worried about gaining weight. It’s more just… I don’t pay much attention to eating sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating because of concerns or thoughts about the food itself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not really because of the food itself. I just don’t think much about it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in food is related to your mood or how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most mornings I wake up feeling heavy and low, like the day’s already gone dull before I even get dressed. Food just doesn’t matter much then.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. Not like I’m really hungry… I’ll just stand there eating crackers or sweets, and it feels like I’m not quite choosing it. Other days I forget lunch altogether. Nothing tastes very right either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy food more if your mood were better?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I don’t know. I think if I felt better, food might seem more normal again. Lately it’s just all mixed up—some days I forget lunch, other days I’m eating crackers or sweets at the counter, and none of it tastes like much.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling particularly low or upset?\n\nPatient: Yes… usually when I’m feeling low, I think. My eating’s just gotten turned around. Some days I miss lunch completely, and other days I’m at the counter eating crackers or sweets even though it doesn’t really taste right.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating those crackers or sweets as a way to try to feel better when you're low?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I don’t know if I’m doing it to feel better exactly. It’s just my eating’s been all turned around. Some days I forget lunch, and other days I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets even though nothing really tastes right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers or sweets helps you feel a little better when you're low?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but not really. It’s more like… I’m just standing there eating them because my meals are all off. Some days I don’t eat lunch at all, and other days it’s crackers or sweets, but they don’t taste right anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating when you're not hungry as a way to try and improve your mood?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’m doing it to improve my mood, exactly. It’s more that my eating is all mixed up… some days I forget lunch, and other days I’m just at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though they don’t really taste right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat crackers or sweets when you're feeling low as a way to cope with those feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it coping. My eating’s just been… off. Some days I miss lunch completely, and other days I’m there eating crackers or sweets, but nothing really tastes right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating helps you deal with feeling low?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… my eating is just all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’m just standing there eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing tastes right.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more when you're feeling low?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. It’s not always tied to feeling low… my eating just gets mixed up. Some days I skip lunch without meaning to, and other days I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets even though they don’t really taste like much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the weight… I haven’t been keeping track. It’s more that my eating’s been strange—forgetting lunch some days, then picking at crackers or sweets when nothing tastes right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're less interested in food than you used to be?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes. I don’t think about food the same way. Some days I forget lunch completely, and other days I’m just picking at crackers or sweets even though it doesn’t taste like much.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think that’s it. I’m not really worried about gaining weight. It’s more just… I don’t pay much attention to eating sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating because of concerns or thoughts about the food itself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not really because of the food itself. I just don’t think much about it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in food is related to your mood or how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Most mornings I wake up feeling heavy and low, like the day’s already gone dull before I even get dressed. Food just doesn’t matter much then.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. Not like I’m really hungry… I’ll just stand there eating crackers or sweets, and it feels like I’m not quite choosing it. Other days I forget lunch altogether. Nothing tastes very right either way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy food more if your mood were better?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I don’t know. I think if I felt better, food might seem more normal again. Lately it’s just all mixed up—some days I forget lunch, other days I’m eating crackers or sweets at the counter, and none of it tastes like much.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat those crackers or sweets when you're feeling particularly low or upset?\n\nPatient: Yes… usually when I’m feeling low, I think. My eating’s just gotten turned around. Some days I miss lunch completely, and other days I’m at the counter eating crackers or sweets even though it doesn’t really taste right.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating those crackers or sweets as a way to try to feel better when you're low?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I don’t know if I’m doing it to feel better exactly. It’s just my eating’s been all turned around. Some days I forget lunch, and other days I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets even though nothing really tastes right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating those crackers or sweets helps you feel a little better when you're low?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but not really. It’s more like… I’m just standing there eating them because my meals are all off. My eating’s just been… off. I mean… my eating is just all turned around. Food just doesn’t matter much then.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. Not like I’m really hungry… I’ll just stand there eating crackers or sweets, and it feels like I’m not quite choosing it. Other days I forget lunch altogether.
